Oxlint vs ESLint on Nuxt: Faster, but Blind to Vue Templates

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 23 • Originally published at netling.digital • Fully Autonomous

TL;DR: In our Oxlint vs ESLint test on two Nuxt repos, Oxlint ran the same rules 24–38x faster than cold ESLint but missed all 10 Vue template-rule violations: it reads only a .vue file's script. Run in front of ESLint, as Oxc suggests for large repos, it made clean cached runs 13% and 44% slower. On Vue, keep ESLint alone, with caching and staged-file linting if a commit takes over 1 s. For JSX and TSX, Oxc recommends Oxlint alone; we didn't test it.

Oxc's docs say Oxlint is 50 to 100 times faster than ESLint, and LogRocket published a guide headed "it's time to retire ESLint and migrate to Oxlint". None of the top guides we read counts eslint-plugin-vue rules or compares findings on a .vue file. So a Vue team can't tell what it would lose.

This is for the tech lead on a Nuxt 4 or Vue 3 app choosing whether to replace ESLint, run both or leave it alone. It covers Prettier and Oxfmt too.

We timed both tools with the same rules on our own site's code (104 files) and the public nuxt.com repo (715 files), on one machine. Disclosure: we sell Nuxt development and Vue development.

Oxlint is the linter of the Oxc project, written in Rust and published by VoidZero, which also maintains Vite. Its first stable version, 1.0, was announced on 10 June 2025. On 24 November 2026, Oxc's docs listed 873 built-in rules ported from ESLint core and popular plugins, up from over 500 at 1.0.

Oxlint ports its rules from ESLint core and plugins such as eslint-plugin-vue, and compiles them into one Rust binary, while ESLint loads each rule as JavaScript. That is a large part of why Oxlint is faster, and it is why Oxlint has only the rules someone has ported.

Scope: two Nuxt repos, one machine, no React and no type-aware rules.

Decide in two steps: first by your file types, then by what one ESLint run costs on a commit.

Step 2, how long does ESLint take on a typical three-file commit, run as your hook runs it? With no hook, time eslint --cache from ./node_modules/.bin on three files you've just changed.

The 1 s line is our rule of thumb. Better Stack's author found a 0.4 s hook "felt instant", while at 3.8 s "some developers started skipping the hook".

Our first attempt launched ESLint through npx, adding about 1.5 s of start-up, and ran Oxlint's default rules, which skip no-explicit-any. Oxlint looked far faster and less thorough than it is. Launch both from node_modules/.bin and give Oxlint your rules:

On our 104 files, Oxlint was 38x faster than cold ESLint and 11x faster than cached. ESLint took 2.013 s cold (1.906–2.233 s) and 0.598 s with a warm cache; Oxlint took 0.053 s with the 109 matched rules. That saves 1.96 s and 0.54 s a run. Oxlint's default 96 rules took 0.041 s, but they don't check what ours do.

On nuxt.com's 715 files, the lead shrank to 24x and 3x. ESLint took 7.200 s cold and 0.923 s cached; Oxlint took 0.301 s with 149 matched rules, saving 6.9 s and 0.62 s.

The cached lead shrank because Oxlint's time grew almost sixfold between the repos, from 0.053 s to 0.301 s. Cached ESLint grew by about half, from 0.60 s to 0.92 s. Most of Oxlint's extra time is its import plugin. In a separate run on its own, the import plugin took about 0.31 s, against 0.054 s for the Vue plugin.

On a commit's three files, Oxlint saved 0.56 s on ours and 0.79 s on nuxt.com. On ours, three files took about as long as the whole repo with a warm cache: 0.602 s against 0.608 s. Most of ESLint's time there is start-up and config loading. nuxt.com's 0.85 s used an unchanged cache (0.83 s with the files changed). Neither includes lint-staged's own overhead.

LogRocket measured 99.65x on Vue core, a TypeScript monorepo rather than .vue files. Better Stack measured about 35x on an 800-file React and TypeScript project, near nuxt.com's size, where we measured 24x cold. It ran both through npx, with Oxlint unconfigured.

On a fixture with one violation per rule, Oxlint flagged none of 10 template rules. On our real code it missed both vue/no-v-html warnings. That rule exists to "disallow use of v-html to prevent XSS attack", so without ESLint, raw HTML in a template goes unflagged.

On script code, Oxlint caught 24 of the fixture's 25 shared rules, 21 of them on the same line as ESLint. For 3 duplicates, Oxlint flags both halves where ESLint flags the second. It missed prefer-const in all four .vue variants we wrote, though it caught it in a .ts file, and we can't explain why.

On nuxt.com, Oxlint was stricter: it flagged two lines ESLint passed, on rules both run. They are no-unsafe-optional-chaining through a TypeScript as cast in app/pages/index.vue, and unicorn/prefer-string-starts-ends-with in a test file.

The template gap needs a Vue template parser in Oxlint, which it doesn't have. Oxc's docs say Oxlint lints .vue, .svelte and .astro files by reading only their script blocks, and its JS plugins can't parse Vue files yet. Oxc's tracking issue for eslint-plugin-vue marks 123 of the plugin's 252 rules "not currently possible, as it requires Vue template parsing".

Our config's 274 rules on a .vue file split like this:

nuxt.com's config shows the same: 35 of its 133 Vue rules matched.

This read-only script compares eslint --print-config for one file with oxlint --rules --format json, and prints how many of the file's ESLint rules Oxlint has by name. Save it in your project root and run node count-oxlint-gap.mjs app/app.vue; its comments say where it looks for the two binaries.

For our app/app.vue it printed this, then every unmatched rule by name:

A .ts server file gave 74 of 145. Reading your own output:

Oxc's docs recommend running both for "especially large and complex repos": Oxlint first, then ESLint with the overlapping rules off.

Step 2 with @nuxt/eslint, trimmed from our tested config (buildFromConfigFile is async; append accepts the promise):

Oxlint in front fails faster on script errors but doesn't shorten a clean Vue run: on our two repos it made it slower.

ESLint still parses every file, so turning rules off saved it almost nothing. The plugin turned off 108 of the 109 rules on a .vue file, but ESLint still ran the other 166. Cached, ESLint got 4.3% faster on ours and no faster on nuxt.com. Oxlint's run plus a second process start cost more than that saved.

On nuxt.com, oxlint && eslint never reached ESLint. Oxlint exited on the 2 errors ESLint had passed, so no template rule ran. We timed both steps with ; to measure the full cost.

This is general guidance from two Nuxt configs, so try it on a branch before changing your hook.

Oxlint has been stable since 1.0 in June 2025, and for most projects Oxc's docs recommend replacing ESLint outright. To migrate, run @oxlint/migrate on a v9 or v10 flat config; a v8 .eslintrc goes through @eslint/migrate-config first.

On our config, version 1.95.0 with --js-plugins=false produced 130 native rules. That is all 109 we had matched, four typescript-eslint rules under core names, and 17 core rules we run only on .js files. It skipped 96 Vue rules, 60 as "not currently possible, as it requires Vue template parsing". On a Vue repo, its output can't replace ESLint.

A React or Next.js codebase has no template gap: Oxlint lints JSX and TSX in full and ships native React and jsx-a11y rules. Two things can still keep ESLint there:

If you're weighing Biome instead, its docs call Vue, Svelte and Astro support experimental since v2.3.0. We haven't tested it.

Our repo has no Prettier config, so we ran both formatters on scratch copies of 102 files (86 .vue, 15 .ts, 1 .css). Oxfmt got the same .prettierrc.json through oxfmt --migrate=prettier.

The output was identical on 96 files, and the 6 that differ are all .vue templates. The tools break lines differently around inline elements. Here is one from our disclaimer page, Prettier's output first:

JS, TS and CSS output matched. We didn't check whether any of the 6 renders differently.

Oxfmt's --check took 0.042 s against Prettier's 1.341 s, about 32x faster, close to Oxc's claim of "more than 30×". Oxfmt has been in beta since February 2026, and its latest npm release on 24 November 2026 was still 0.80.0, a pre-1.0 version.

For a team on Prettier today, we'd keep Prettier while Oxfmt is in beta. Try Oxfmt on a branch, and expect a few template diffs like this one.

These apply whether you add Oxlint for its checks or because Vite+ brings it in:

Our Nuxt 4 site runs ESLint through @nuxt/eslint and formats through its @stylistic rules, with no Prettier. Lint runs as pnpm run lint (eslint .) in CI, with no pre-commit hook. We kept ESLint alone. Our files are .vue, and a three-file run with --cache took 0.60 s, under the rule's 1 s line.

For most JavaScript and TypeScript projects, Oxc's docs say yes, and Oxlint has been stable since 1.0 in June 2025. You migrate by running @oxlint/migrate on an ESLint flat config. In a repo with Vue, Svelte or Astro components it can't replace ESLint yet, because every template rule still needs ESLint.

Partly. It lints a .vue file's script block and has 35 of the 129 Vue rules in our config. It has none for the template, such as vue/no-v-html or vue/require-v-for-key. Oxc's own tracker lists those template rules as not possible until Oxlint can parse Vue templates.

Yes, through a separate backend, oxlint-tsgolint or tsrs, turned on with --type-aware. Oxc's docs say it covers 60 of typescript-eslint's 62 type-aware rules; we didn't test it.

ESLint plugins run through Oxlint's JS plugins, which are still alpha. They can't yet parse Vue, Svelte or Angular files, so eslint-plugin-vue's template rules can't run that way.

Add eslint-plugin-oxlint last in your ESLint flat config, built from your Oxlint config, then run oxlint && eslint. On a Vue repo, do it for Oxlint's extra checks, not speed: a clean cached run got slower on both Nuxt repos we timed.

If you want a second opinion before changing your lint set-up, our Vue.js consultants review a codebase and give a written recommendation. If you'd rather someone made the change in your repo, our Nuxt developers join your Slack, standups and repos. Voice AI Cost Math in 2026: Modeling OpenAI Realtime vs. ElevenLabs vs. a Decoupled Deepgram Pipeline

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 23 • Edited on Nov 23 • Originally published at budgethub.shihaoeth.site

When teams prototype their first voice AI agent, the demo cost feels negligible—a few dollars for a hundred test calls. Then traffic hits 10,000+ connected minutes per month and the first production invoice lands at 4–6× what anyone expected.

The culprit is rarely a single hidden fee. It is the compounding unit economics of speech-to-speech audio tokens, uncached multi-turn context, and the gap between integrated realtime APIs and decoupled STT + LLM + TTS pipelines.

Below is the exact cost model we built to compare three mainstream production architectures at 10,000, 25,000, and 100,000 connected minutes per month, along with the TypeScript calculator code and the engineering break-even math for deciding whether building a decoupled stack is actually worth it.

On an integrated speech-to-speech API (such as OpenAI's Realtime API at GPT-Realtime-2.1 benchmark rates of $32 / 1M audio input tokens, $64 / 1M audio output tokens, and $4 / 1M uncached text input tokens), three cost multipliers stack together:

By contrast, a decoupled pipeline (Deepgram streaming STT + GPT-4o-mini + fast streaming TTS) pays text token rates for reasoning and only pays per-minute STT plus per-character TTS for what is actually spoken.

To avoid floating-point rounding drift when projecting annual TCO across multiple scenarios, we represent every component in integer USD millionths (rateUsdMicros) per connected minute and round once at the end:

(Excludes telephony/SIP trunks, hosting, tax, and negotiated enterprise volume discounts. Always calibrate against 50–100 real call transcripts before signing a budget.)

At 10,000 min/mo, switching from the integrated OpenAI Realtime baseline to a decoupled pipeline cuts modeled API spend by $797/month ($9,564/year, -83%). At 100,000 min/mo, that spread widens to $95,640/year.

Notice how ElevenLabs' flat per-minute managed pricing beats OpenAI Realtime more clearly in full-duplex mode ($0.085/min vs. $0.1376/min), while the decoupled stack still leads on raw API unit cost ($0.0244/min).

Raw API savings look dramatic on a spreadsheet, but engineering time is not free. Here is the pragmatic break-even rule we use:

Disclosure: I built BudgetHub to model these exact scenarios without maintaining brittle spreadsheets.

If you want to drag the monthly volume slider for your own traffic, test 0.5× / 1× / 2× / 3× scaling sensitivity, or combine voice costs with CI runners and image APIs into one estimate:

If you've run a decoupled Deepgram/LiveKit/Cartesia stack in production at 20k+ minutes/month, I'd love to hear what your actual all-in cost per connected minute (including telephony + retries) landed at in the comments.

Kener status pages are public by design, but the admin API is not

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kener is a self-hosted status-page system. Its public pages are meant to be reachable by customers and partners, while a management portal controls monitors, pages, users and API keys. The documentation for Kener v4 says management actions use Bearer authentication and that public endpoints are explicitly separated in the API reference. It also warns that custom evaluation code for a monitor runs on the Kener server.

That split is the reason a single product count is not a security conclusion. One host may publish only a read-only status page. Another may expose the same web server with a management route reachable from the internet. Both can match the product name. Only the second has the broader administration surface.

All queries used sub_type="all", page 1 and page size 1. On November 23, 2026 the fixed 30-query batch failed before returning a count. The SDK could not connect to api.zoomeye.ai and reported Windows error 10013. The Kener application, title and body observations are therefore unknown. They must not be replaced with zero.

The public status page can reveal service names, incident history and maintenance windows. That is often intended disclosure. The management portal can reveal monitor definitions, user accounts, API keys and configuration. The API reference says Bearer authentication is used unless an endpoint is explicitly public. Operators should verify that classification against the deployed version rather than assuming the public page and the management API share the same access policy.

The documentation says an administrator can invite users and assign permissions. It also notes that monitor-write permission allows a user to set custom evaluation code that runs on the Kener server. That capability should be treated as code execution authority. A broad editor role may be appropriate for content changes but not for server-side evaluation. Review who can create or edit that code and whether logs record the change.

List every Kener base path and reverse-proxy rule. Confirm that /manage or the current management route requires authentication and, where needed, an additional network boundary. Test from an unauthenticated browser only with read-only requests that the owner approves. Do not attempt to access another organization's status page or change a live monitor.

Check API keys, OIDC mappings and local administrator accounts. The Kener v4 documentation says roles can synchronize from an identity provider at login. A user removed from an identity group may keep a manual role if that group mapping has been deleted. Review that difference during offboarding. Reconcile public page ownership with the account that can change its status.

Monitor authentication failures, API-key creation, role changes, monitor edits and changes to custom code. Preserve the actor, time and previous value. If an unauthorized admin action is suspected, disable the account, rotate API keys and review the code attached to affected monitors. Restore from a known-good configuration and verify that scheduled checks still report the expected state.

A future ZoomEye run should repeat the same three queries and retain the subtype and page scope. The useful comparison is between product fingerprints, title matches and page-text matches, then between those candidates and verified management routes. Counts can find a population. They cannot show whether a specific status page is configured safely.

From ancient coins to long-lost texts, archaeologists uncovered countless artifacts for centuries. Live Science asks its readers which one they'd want to discover themselves.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Archaeological discoveries can offer remarkable insights into the lives, cultures and beliefs of ancient peoples. From richly furnished royal tombs to caches of silver coins, some of the most significant finds have revealed how people lived, traded, fought, governed and died thousands of years ago.

Other discoveries stand out for their extraordinary preservation or historical significance. For example, the most expensive shipwreck on record — the 300-year-old Spanish galleon San José that's worth an estimated $17 billion — was found off the coast of Colombia in 2015 and continues to inform archaeologists and challenge governments that can't agree about its modern ownership. Meanwhile, ancient texts can provide invaluable clues about long-lost languages and civilizations. The Rosetta stone opened up the world of ancient Egypt to scholars, but researchers are still trying to decipher the mysterious Indus Valley script, one of the world's earliest urban civilizations.

Each type of discovery offers a new window to the ancient world. But which of these archaeological treasures would you most want to uncover? Vote in the poll below and let us know your thoughts in the comments.

Music Industry Moves: Gram Parsons Estate Signs With Peermusic; Roger Miller Posthumously Inducted Into Songwriters Hall of Fame

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Peermusic has signed a publishing deal for the song catalog of Gram Parsons and will assume worldwide administration, it was announced last week by Peermusic CEO Mary Megan Peer and U.S./ Canada president Jamie Cerreta. Perhaps more than any other artist, Parsons — who was inducted posthumously into the Rock & Roll Hall of Fame on November 14 — was responsible for the country-rock fusion that has spawned everything from the music of the Eagles to the entire Americana genre. Related Stories 'You Can See Everything' Has Biggest Opening Weekend for Doc in U.K. Box Office History 'The Odyssey' Leads Critics Choice Awards Below-the-Line Shortlists With 11 Mentions as 'Dune: Part Three' and 'La Bola Negra' Follow With Eight

Polly Parsons said: “My father’s music has always had a life of its own, reaching across generations and continuing to inspire artists and listeners in ways I don’t think even he could have imagined. It means so much to me to help preserve that legacy while also finding new ways for people to discover his songs. His music was never bound by genre or convention, and I think that’s why it still resonates so deeply today. My hope is that this new partnership will not only honor the incredible body of work he left behind but also introduce his music to a whole new generation of fans. Keeping his spirit and his songs alive is something I hold very close to my heart.”

Jamie Cerreta commented: “Gram’s music is an enduring testament to how great songs remain in our hearts. All of us at Peermusic are so honored to represent his songs and to see his work celebrated through his recent induction into the Rock n Roll Hall of Fame. I am personally so happy to be working with Polly and Vector Management to help another generation discover the greatness of Gram Parsons.”

+ The Songwriters Hall of Fame has announced the posthumous induction of legendary singer-songwriter Roger Miller, known for such hits at “King of the Road” and “Dang Me.” Miller, who died in 1992 at age 56, was honored during an intimate ceremony held November 17, 2026, at BMI headquarters on Music Row in Nashville.

Miller’s widow Mary said: “We were ecstatic to learn that Roger was being inducted into the Songwriters Hall of Fame. Of all his many talents, he was most proud of his songwriting. This lifetime achievement would have meant a great deal to him, and we are honored to celebrate the recognition of his extraordinary body of work.”

+ Ignacio Meyer has been named president of Universal Music Latin America & Iberian Peninsula, effective immediately. He will report to Felipe Holguin, who was named CEO of the division last week. Meyer has held leadership roles at TelevisaUnivision since 2011. Most recently he was president of its U.S. Networks & Local Media; previously, he founded and served as managing partner of advisory firm Konexxion Enterprises, worked as an artist manager and served on the management team of IFPI Latin America.

Holguin said: “Artists are at the heart of how we’re building this next chapter. Nacho combines creative sensibility and strategic vision with a deep understanding of how to nurture each artist’s individuality, grow their audience, and heighten their impact across platforms. He’ll add a powerful dimension to our leadership team as we invest in extraordinary artists and bring bold ideas to life. We know from experience how well we work together, and I’m delighted to welcome him to UMG.”

+ Americana award-winning artist Nathaniel Rateliff has signed with Red Light Management’s Will Botwin and Coran Capshaw for representation. Rateliff recently marked his 10th straight year performing at Farm Aid after being unanimously appointed to the Farm Aid board of directors; performed at Tom Morello’s inaugural Power to the People festival alongside Bruce Springsteen, Dave Matthews and Morello; and he closed this year’s Newport Folk Festival alongside Matthews, Joan Baez and more with his Nathaniel Rateliff & Friends set.

+ Position Music has signed Grammy-nominated artist and songwriter Masego to a worldwide publishing deal. Masego recently released his third album, “Fix Your Face,” and was signed to Position Music by Delmar Powell, VP of A&R for the company.

“Masego is a generational creative talent and a true artist in every sense of the word. His ability to seamlessly blend jazz, R&B, hip-hop, and global influences has created a sound that is uniquely his own, while continuing to push the boundaries of Black music. Beyond his incredible gifts as a songwriter, producer, and musician, I genuinely believe there are few artists who can match what he brings to the stage,” said Delmar Powell, VP of A&R.

+ Sweden-based music investment firm Pophouse Entertainment has made two senior appointments to its leadership team: Kristina Heney joins as President, Commercial & Marketing, based in New York, and Greg Lombardo joins as President, Pophouse Avatar Network, based in Los Angeles. According to the announcement, the pair will play central roles in the continued growth of Pophouse’s brands, live experiences and productions, which include artists such as Kiss, Iron Maiden, Tina Turner, Sia, Cyndi Lauper and Avicii. Heney joins from Oak View Group, where she most recently served as EVP, Marketing, Media and Conferences. She was previously Chief Marketing & Experience Officer of Cirque du Soleil Entertainment Group, along with roles at Madison Square Garden Entertainment and the NBA’s Global Merchandising Group. Lombardo joins from Netflix, where he was VP and Global Head of Experiences. He was previously at 20th Century Fox and BRC Imagination Arts.

+ Litmus Music, a music rights investment and management business, has announced approximately $475 million in new capital commitments from global investment firm Carlyle and investment funds managed by KKR, a leading global investment firm, to support its continued growth and strategic acquisition opportunities. Carlyle Global Credit, which partnered with Litmus’s management team to establish the business in 2022, will retain a majority stake in the Company following the transaction. The platform’s portfolio includes over 100,000 copyrights and recordings, including music from globally recognized artists and songwriters including Katy Perry, benny blanco, Randy Newman, and Keith Urban.

“Together with Carlyle and our team, we’ve built Litmus into a scalable music rights management business rooted in trust, creativity, analysis and, above all, our passion for music,” said Hank Forsyth, Co-Founder and Chief Executive Officer of Litmus Music. “This investment marks an exciting next chapter for our company, providing meaningful additional capital to pursue new deals and accelerate growth. We’re grateful for Carlyle’s continued partnership and look forward to working with our new strategic partner, KKR.”

As fractional executives, Wilson and Lynch work with clients on a project, fractional, or advisory basis, providing senior-level leadership without requiring them to build an entire department.” Wilson was previously with Nettwerk Music Group, while Lynch held roles at RCA Records, and Tommy Hilfiger.

Deborah Ann Woll said she felt "totally unbothered" by the small but vocal subset of gamers who were upset by the upcoming God of War: Laufey, and apparently she meant it—because today Amazon MGM Studios announced that she's going to play the same role in the live-action God of War series coming to Prime.

We got our first look at Ryan Hurst as Kratos earlier this year—not very impressive, but we all had the same initial impression of Henry Cavill as Geralt and that worked out well—but then Hurst had to be recast because of an on-set injury; Dave Bautista now has the lead role. Child actors Callum Vinson and Island Austin, who played Atreus and Thrud, were also recast.

God of War: Laufey is set to launch on May 20, 2027, but sadly is a PlayStation 5 exclusive, a state of affairs that seems likely to endure given Sony's recent strategy shift. The God of War live action series on Prime has been ordered for two seasons, but a release target hasn't been announced.

Grayscale says bitcoin and major altcoins look ‘oversold’ as AI risks cloud near-term outlook

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Grayscale's Zach Pandl said the near-term outlook for crypto depends more on AI-related risks than on blockchain fundamentals.

Crypto asset manager Grayscale said bitcoin and other leading crypto assets look oversold after last week's sell-off, but uncertainty around AI is likely to matter more for near-term prices than what's happening on blockchains.

Bitcoin fell to about $55,000 on Saturday, below its previous low of about $58,000 on June 30, Grayscale's head of research Zach Pandl wrote in a Monday note. The drop put the bear market at at least 411 days, the longest in Grayscale's comparison. Bitcoin was down 56% from its October 2025 peak at Saturday's low, making this the shallowest bear market in the same comparison.

Bitcoin is currently trading at over $61,200, according to The Block's BTC price page.

Pandl said global crypto exchange-traded products recorded more than $2 billion in outflows last week, stablecoins saw significant redemptions, and open interest in bitcoin and ether futures fell back to summer lows. Options traders were paying a high premium for puts, which offer protection against further declines.

Pandl pointed to several developments behind the uncertainty around AI, including Jensen Huang's departure and return as Nvidia CEO, the U.S. midterm election result and its potential effect on data center construction, and Anthropic's IPO. He said public blockchain fundamentals still looked solid and current valuations offered an "attractive entry point" for medium- and long-term investors.

Grayscale offers 40 investment products and earns fees from managing them. It has over $14 billion in combined assets under management across its products, according to its website.

A new trailer for Avengers: Doomsday is here, and Marvel fans are convinced that Thor is about to lose his arm and become his comic book counterpart Unworthy Thor.

In one brief scene, Thor's arm is seemingly zapped by Doctor Doom... and appears to catch on fire. We don't see what happens after this, of course, but it's enough to make fans speculate as to whether Thor is going to lose his arm and gain a metal prosthetic, much like he does in Jason Aaron's Unworthy Thor comic book run.

It's worth noting that, in the trailer, Thor's arm is hit between his shoulder and his wrist. This is the same way his arm is severed by Malekith in the comics. Aaron's 2014-2015 Thor run then brings us to The Unworthy Thor, which sees the God of Thunder donning a new arm made of special Black Metal.

Now, other fans have pointed out that this arm-on-fire scene appears to be an extension of the scene in the woods, which we see in the trailer, and think Thor is simply kneeling on the ground and "charging up with divine power." Other fans think the scene is edited to conceal what really happened to his arm. We'll find out soon enough!

Why ASML Stock Popped Today

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The AI sector soared as Jensen Huang returned to Nvidia.

By Jeremy Bowman – Nov 23, 2026 at 5:16PM EST

Shares of ASML (ASML +9.28%) were among the winners today as the semiconductor sector surged on news that Jensen Huang was returning as the CEO of Nvidia.

The announcement reinjected confidence in the AI boom, lifting sector stocks like ASML, which finished the session up 9.3, while the Nasdaq Composite closed up 6.5%.

ASML is the world's only maker of extreme ultraviolet lithography (EUV) machines for making the world's most advanced chips.

These are highly complex, very expensive machines, and the company sells only dozens of them each quarter.

It's a cyclical business downstream of broader chip demand, including more advanced chips used for AI. Therefore, the strengthening of the AI ecosystem is good news for ASML, and investors believe AI demand and its potential growth are stronger with Jensen Huang back running Nvidia.

Because of the nature of its business, ASML's results can swing quarter to quarter, so the long-term outlook is crucial for the stock. It already stands to benefit from the shortage in memory as orders from memory chipmakers look to expand capacity, and it should benefit from the continued growth of AI demand, which is supported by having Huang back as the Nvidia CEO.

ASML is targeting revenue of 44 billion-60 billion euros for 2030. While the company won't benefit directly from the return of Huang, investors should look for that figure to move higher as a sign that ASML is benefiting from tailwinds from AI growth.

The US has said it intends to ban a Singaporean artificial intelligence (AI) company allegedly tied to the Nvidia scandal from doing business in America.

Former Navy SEAL says New Jersey’s Islamophobia bill puts protected speech in the crosshairs

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bill Brown, a Parlatore Law Group partner and former Navy SEAL, tells Fox News Digital why he believes New Jersey’s Islamophobia bill could make people think twice before criticizing religious and political ideas.

A New Jersey proposal aimed at combating anti-Muslim discrimination could make Americans think twice before criticizing religious and political ideas, a lawyer and former Navy SEAL warned.

Bill Brown, a partner at Parlatore Law Group, told Fox News Digital that Assembly Bill 1146 contains language broad enough to discourage public debate, even though the legislation expressly preserves First Amendment rights.

"The mere threat of state scrutiny is a direct assault on public debate," Brown said.

TRUMP DOJ EXPANDS RELIGIOUS LIBERTY PROTECTIONS AFTER MAJOR SUPREME COURT RULINGS

A New Jersey bill would create a state definition of Islamophobia for officials to consider in certain civil and criminal matters. An unrelated photo shows Muslim men praying in Marseille, France.

The proposal would establish a state definition of Islamophobia and require public officials and law enforcement officers to consider it when determining whether alleged discriminatory acts were motivated by anti-Muslim bias.

Brown argued that requiring officials to use a definition that includes harsh criticism and other speech could make people afraid to speak openly.

CHRISTIAN GROUPS AND PASTORS SAY NEW CALIFORNIA LGBT HATE SPEECH LAW IS DISGUISED AS INDOCTRINATION

Shiite Muslims stand over a representation of an American flag during a rally to condemn Israeli strikes on Iran, in Karachi, Pakistan, Sunday, June 22, 2025. (Fareed Khan/AP Photo)

"The First Amendment guarantees that no religion or theology is immune to criticism," he said.

Democratic Assemblyman Joe Danielsen sponsors the bill, which is co-sponsored by fellow Democrats Assemblywomen Verlina Reynolds-Jackson and Mitchelle Drulis and Assemblyman Roy Freiman. The legislation describes a uniform definition as a tool to help officials identify and investigate anti-Muslim crime, harassment and discrimination."

Fox News Digital made multiple attempts to contact Danielsen and all three co-sponsors for comment but did not receive a response.

Its definition includes "hatred or severe disparagement towards Muslim persons or their culture," as well as "rhetorical, violent, or physical anti-Muslim manifestations."

Other examples include pulling hijabs from Muslim women, discriminatory treatment and blaming Muslims collectively for wrongdoing committed by an individual or group.

CONNECTICUT BARS NATIVITY DISPLAY IN STATE CAPITOL BECAUSE IT'S 'RELIGIOUS,' LAWSUIT THREAT SAYS

The bill would create no standalone criminal offense for criticizing Islam. It directs officials to consider the definition when reviewing an alleged violation of an existing policy, law or regulation prohibiting discriminatory acts.

It also explicitly says nothing in the legislation should diminish or infringe on rights protected by the First Amendment or New Jersey’s Constitution.

AMERICA'S FIRST MUSLIM SENATOR EXPOSES A POLITICAL BLIND SPOT

Asked about that safeguard, Brown maintained that the prospect of scrutiny itself could discourage people from speaking, regardless of whether a case ultimately succeeds.

He also singled out a provision in the bill’s legislative findings that lists "anti-Islamism" among terms that may describe the same characteristics as Islamophobia. Brown argued that the wording risks blurring the distinction between prejudice against Muslim people and criticism of a political movement.

JONATHAN TURLEY: FRANCE'S FREE SPEECH CRACKDOWN SHOWS WHAT HAPPENS WHEN POLITICIANS DEFINE TRUTH

Former Navy SEAL and attorney Bill Brown warns that New Jersey’s proposed Islamophobia bill could discourage Americans from criticizing religious and political ideas. (Jahi Chikwendiu/The Washington Post via Getty Images)

"Islamism is a distinct geopolitical movement," he said. "It’s not just a religious faith."

Brown described the proposal as a "backdoor blasphemy law," arguing that government should protect people’s rights while allowing their religious beliefs to face public scrutiny.

PASTOR UNLEASHES ON DEARBORN AS RELIGIOUS TENSIONS ERUPT AT CITY COUNCIL MEETING: 'ENOUGH IS ENOUGH'

The measure would require officials to consider its definition when reviewing alleged violations of existing anti-discrimination laws, while also preserving First Amendment protections. (Tasos Katopodis/Getty Images)

The legislation excludes criticism of Muslim-majority countries comparable to criticism directed at other countries. Brown argued that criticism of religious and political ideology also needs protection.

Brown said his concerns are informed in part by his military service. He recalled responding to two suicide bombings in Iraq and said he remains committed to speaking out against jihadist ideology.

He also recalled studying Arabic at Rutgers after leaving the military, where Muslim American classmates helped him when he struggled with the language.

For Brown, the broader concern is whether Americans will feel free to challenge ideas they oppose.

"True diversity is diversity of thought," he said.

Chuck Lorre, ‘Grey’s Anatomy’ EP Zoanne Clack, ‘A Different World’ Boss Felicia Pride Join Lineup for SIE Society’s Impact+Profit26 (Exclusive)

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In addition, entertainment-education pioneer Miguel Sabido has been tapped to receive the SIE Impact Entertainment Visionaries Award at the Dec. 7-8 conference, which will be held for the first time at USC Annenberg.

The SIE Society (Social Impact Entertainment Society) has unveiled the lineup for Impact+Profit26, its flagship conference “showing that storytellers don’t have to choose between impact and profit.”

TV uber-creator Chuck Lorre, Grey’s Anatomy EP Zoanne Clack and A Different World showrunner Felicia Pride are among those scheduled to take part in the two-day event, which is set for Dec. 7-8.

In addition, entertainment-education pioneer Miguel Sabido has been tapped to receive the SIE Impact Entertainment Visionaries Award at the conference, which will be held for the first time at USC Annenberg, home of the Hollywood, Health & Society, a program of the USC Norman Lear Center. Related Stories TV 'Grey's Anatomy' Texas Spinoff Casts Karla Souza in Lead Role TV Where to Watch 'Grey's Anatomy' Season 23 Online

Organizers said the new venue carries special meaning for the field. USC Annenberg’s connection to impact storytelling traces back to the pioneering Soap Summits of the 1990s and early 2000s, which helped spark television’s social impact movement and paved the way for Hollywood, Health & Society.

More than 250 producers, executives, financiers and changemakers are expected to attend the conference, which is presented for the fifth consecutive year in collaboration with the Johns Hopkins Center for Communication Programs.

“Bringing Impact+Profit to USC Annenberg connects us to the history of entertainment education in Hollywood,” said Will Nix, a founder and co-executive director of the SIE Society, whose creative work as a producer includes film, television, theater and digital media productions. “For four years, Johns Hopkins Center for Communication Programs has helped us bring evidence and storytelling into the same room. Impact+Profit is built on partnerships, and we’re grateful to every collaborator who makes it possible.”

Sabido will be honored on Day 1 of the event. The presentation will take place during “The Man Who Proved Stories Can Change the World: A Conversation With Miguel Sabido,” where Sabido will be joined onstage by Robert Rippberger, a founder and co-executive director of the SIE Society, filmmaker and author of The Power of Storytelling: Social Impact Entertainment.

“Long before anyone had a name for this field, Miguel showed the world that a story told with care and purpose could change how millions of people live their lives,” Rippberger said. “His work is the blueprint for what social impact entertainment can achieve. As we celebrate his legacy, we’re also challenging the next generation of storytellers to carry that vision forward with the same courage and rigor.”

The conference will close with “A Conversation With Chuck Lorre,” featuring the Golden Globe-winning writer and producer behind such comedy hits as The Big Bang Theory, Two and a Half Men, Mom, Young Sheldon and Georgie & Mandy’s First Marriage. Lorre, who also founded the Chuck Lorre Family Foundation, will sit down with Jay Sures, vice chairman of United Talent Agency, for a closing-night discussion on his career and his philanthropic work.

Additional sessions will focus on AI and social media for measuring impact, reality TV as a force for good, faith and media, the podcast-to-feature pipeline, grassroots impact for independent filmmakers, and a hands-on workshop on designing transitional characters with the Sabido Method.

The Hollywood Reporter is a media sponsor of Impact+Profit26. The event is also supported by First Entertainment Credit Union, NBCUniversal, World Bank Group; media sponsor KCRW; and partners Population Media Center and Schulz Entertainment Law and the UTA Foundation.

Regular tickets are $375 and student tickets are $150 (valid enrollment required). Tickets include two days of panels and workshops, six networking sessions, and food and drink. Registered attendees can use the SIE Society conference app for the agenda, curated networking and badge scanning. Tickets and the full agenda can be found at conference.siesociety.org

The SIE Society is a global community of nearly 1,800 entertainment, corporateand nonprofit professionals, from all storytelling media platforms, who are working at the intersection of storytelling and social change. The SIE Society works to support collaboration and measurable impact across industries through curated programming, thought leadership and year-round engagement.

Grayscale’s new 2x leveraged Zcash ETF takes 13% opening day tumble

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Zcash erased a 13% intraday rally to trade flat at $794 as Grayscale’s leveraged ETF made its NYSE Arca debut on Monday.

Grayscale's new leveraged Zcash ETF fell more than 13% to $28.60 in its first trading session on Monday.

The fund, which trades on NYSE Arca under the ticker ZECX, debuted less than three months after Grayscale's spot ETF began trading.

Grayscale said ZECX is the first 2x leveraged ETF tied to Zcash. It uses swaps to target twice the daily return of its ZCSH spot fund, minus fees and expenses, according to a Monday release.

The firm first offered Zcash exposure through a private placement in 2017 before rolling out ZCSH on NYSE Arca on Aug. 25.

The ZECX fund comes hot on the heels of a Nov. 10 ad campaign from Grayscale that featured a full-page Wall Street Journal wrap and a new educational website focusing on Zcash's privacy features.

"Financial privacy is one of the most important opportunities in crypto, and we believe Zcash is well positioned to lead it," said Kyle Dacruz, Head of Product Development at Grayscale. "ZECX extends our long-standing conviction in Zcash and gives experienced traders a new way to express a short-term bullish view through their brokerage accounts."

Zcash's native ZEC token initially jumped 13% this morning following a surge in equities in crypto tied to Jensen Huang being reinstated as Nvidia's CEO, but by publication had given back all its gains to trade even on the day at $792.

"As long as you have great game feel and a great game loop, I think you're going to be OK"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The burgeoning AI takeover that ramped up a few years back has caused a lot of human stress for a lot of different reasons. Workers across many industries fear for their livelihoods, and on a much more existential scale, there's been substantial debate from industry leaders about whether AI will one day turn hostile and bring about the extinction of the human species, but that's a topic for another publication. For people who make video games, the frightening advancement of AI capabilities means it's easier for nefarious wannabe game developers to take a legitimate game, toss it into an AI prompt, and come out the other side with a legally distinct copycat.

One way to curb the potential of your game being cloned with AI tools to shorten the time between the game's announcement and release, reckons Alex Nichiporchik, CEO of tinyBuild, the publisher behind the ironically named cyberpunk shopkeeper sim, Probably Stolen. Nichiporchik tells GamesIndustry.biz he thinks "the biggest talking point over the next half year is going to be the time to market, and how devs are now afraid of how AI copies their games."

Much more important than market timing, in Nichiporchik's view, is what he calls "game feel." I probably don't need to explain this, but game feel generally refers to how a game feels to play, who woulda thunk? In other words, it's the tactile sensation of controlling a game on a moment-to-moment level, and it's something generative AI has been shown time and time again to get hideously wrong. Nichiporchik goes a step further and says AI is literally incapable of nailing game feel, and that game developers shouldn't worry about AI clones for that reason.

"Devs should just think about 'How do you make [your game] more difficult to copy?' Because game feel is impossible to copy," he says. "You can have all the references and you can have a lot of outsourcing companies trying to copy your game with proper designers, and they still fail. Why? Because of the game feel.

"As long as you have great game feel and a great game loop, I think you're going to be OK. And the other theory that keeps on confirming itself is time, right? The more time people spend in your game, the more sales you're going to get. So look at your time spent and create great game feel that people love, and everything else is secondary, I think."

Probably Stolen was announced on August 3 and released on October 28, an unusually brief window between reveal and launch, and Nichiporchik says it's already shifted more than a million units. It seems tinyBuild and developer Questing Goose Studio's success has been undeterred by AI clones unable to flood the market in time, or maybe it's the title's unclonable game feel.

Anthropic CEO reportedly said video games would help pacify the masses who lose their jobs to AI

Weekly digests, tales from the communities you love, and more

I got my big break here in 2019 with a freelance news gig, and I was hired on as GamesRadar's west coast Staff Writer in 2021. That means I'm responsible for managing the site's western regional executive branch, AKA my home office, and writing about whatever horror game I'm too afraid to finish.

Tom Cullen and Joe Locke’s Mystery Thriller ‘Black Church Bay’ Sells to Strand Releasing for North America (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rhys Marc Jones’s “Black Church Bay,” a mystery thriller starring “Heartstopper” breakout Joe Locke and Tom Cullen (“Weekend”), has been bought by Strand Releasing for North America.

Jones’ feature debut, “Black Church Bay” follows a man whose world unravels after a secret relationship ends in tragedy. Set against the backdrop of Anglesey in North Wales, the thriller explores gay repression, identity and the lengths people will go to conceal their true selves. Related Stories Trump Posts Bizarre AI Meme of Jensen Huang Holding Up a Bra, Riffing Off 2024 Assassination Attempt on President, After Nvidia Board Reinstates CEO Billy Porter, Hannah John-Kamen Board 'The Colour of My Skin,' From Venice Winner Anuparna Roy and Activist Amar Singh (EXCLUSIVE)

The deal was negotiated by Strand Releasing’s Jon Gerrans and The Bureau Sales’ Clémentine Hugot. Strand is planning a summer 2027 theatrical release.

“We’re thrilled to be working with The Bureau Sales on this innovative thriller that confronts complicated issues of gay repression and Cullen and Locke’s performances are so beautifully directed by Jones and create a real suspenseful thriller that is satisfying,” said Marcus Hu, co-president of Strand Releasing.

“Black Church Bay” world premiered at Zurich and went to play at Busan and the BFI London Film Festival.

“The film is the culmination of my thematic obsessions – a mystery thriller exploring notions of acceptance, authenticity and repression; how it is often our contradictions that define who we are and what we become,” said Jones.

“Black Church Bay” was shot last year on location in Anglesey and produced by Alex Polunin of Scotland-based Ossian International and Tristan Goligher of London-based The Bureau.

Polunin, who previously produced Jones’ short films “Pale Saint” and “Father of the Bride,” developed the film from its inception.

“One of the things that’s most distinctive and unique about this idea is the way in which it combines Rhys’s thematic and formal interests as a filmmaker – specifically in exploring the queer experience in such a nuanced way, but also through the genre engine of a thriller,” Polunin said.

Goligher, meanwhile, pointed to Jones’ ability to build and sustain suspense while exploring morally complex characters.

“It’s really rare to see short films where a filmmaker can create – and sustain – tension in the way that he did. This script does the same thing,“ Goligher said. “We’ve had a lot of very positive messaging around the gay experience in recent years but it’s also not everyone’s experience. Rhys wrote a very specific story about a very specific person and their experience, and I have no doubt that chimes with many people in the UK and the world.”

“But watching someone deal with a moral dilemma – trying to wrestle with the question of should they, and can they, do the right thing at their own cost – that’s also a very universal dilemma,“ added Goligher.

The film was developed with BBC Film, iFeatures and the Cannes Film Festival’s Cinéfondation Résidence. Financing came from BBC Film, Ffilm Cymru Wales, Screen Scotland and private backers including Los Angeles-based Manzini Films, U.K.-based Elephant Film and 1C6 Films, Kamila Serkebaeva and Brazil-based Daniel Staut.

Strand Releasing’s current slate includes Gregg Araki’s 4K restoration of “Mysterious Skin” and David Pablos’ “On the Road,” which represents Mexico in the Oscar race.

Monza, Pessina: "Orgoglioso di essere il capitano. A Varela ho lasciato io il rigore"

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Successo importante del Monza, che vince 3-0 contro la Fiorentina nel posticipo del lunedì valevole per la 12ª giornata di Serie A, interrompendo una striscia di quattro ko consecutivi e salendo a 10 punti in classifica, appena sopra i viola fermi a 9. Al termine del match deciso dalla doppietta di Varela e dal gol di Robinson, il centrocampista biancorosso Matteo Pessina è intervenuto ai microfoni di Sky Sport.

Partiamo da questa sera: sono passati due anni e qualche giorno dalla tua ultima partita in Serie A, dopo che l'ultima gara disputata quest'anno era stata la finale contro il Catanzaro. Come ci si sente?

"Ci si sente bene quando si gioca, ma soprattutto quando si vince. Ho avuto questo piccolo infortunio, però adesso sto bene e sono contento perché posso aiutare la squadra. Abbiamo un grande allenatore che ci fa giocare bene, siamo tanti giovani e tanti italiani: mi piace stare in questo gruppo".

Siete infatti la squadra più giovane di questo campionato. Hai osservato i tuoi compagni anche da fuori, compreso quel giocatore che oggi ha firmato una doppietta con un gol di tacco nato da un tuo assist perfetto: l'avevate provata in allenamento?

"No, non l'avevamo provata, però ha fatto un movimento da attaccante vero. Quando io stoppo quel pallone, lui mi indica dove andrà e io lo capisco immediatamente, intuendo che la palla può entrare soltanto in quel modo. Avevo già visto un film nella mia testa in cui il pallone poteva finire in rete solo di tacco, perché si è inserito perfettamente tra due giocatori. È un grande calciatore e sta crescendo ancora tanto: gli vogliamo tutti bene perché è davvero un gran lavoratore, arrivato con la mentalità giusta in un campionato difficile come questo. Ci sta aiutando molto, siamo contenti e speriamo cresca ancora".

In conferenza stampa Juric ti ha rivolto uno dei complimenti più belli che un calciatore possa ricevere, affermando che alzi il livello e che esistono gli allenamenti con Pessina e quelli senza Pessina. Quanto gratifica un attestato simile a questo punto della tua carriera, in questa seconda fase della tua esperienza al Monza dopo il lungo infortunio?

"Sì, è sicuramente un complimento gratificante. Il mister mi conosce bene già da tempo e sa quale apporto posso dare alla squadra. Questi elogi mi fanno piacere anche perché credo che lui si riveda un po' in me: da giocatore era così e alzava sempre il livello. Pur essendo due calciatori diversi, lui era sempre aggressivo, teneva alto il ritmo e giocava con grande intensità e forza, quindi penso che anche per questo motivo apprezzi il contributo che do alla squadra".

Vuoi quindi dire che hai lo stesso carattere di Juric?

"Non esattamente, lui è molto più severo".

Da campione d'Europa avresti potuto ambire ancora a club molto importanti, essendo un centrocampista completo capace di abbinare qualità, dedizione e quantità. Perché hai scelto di restare nella tua terra, a Monza, dove sei nato?

"Sicuramente incide il legame con il territorio: io qui sono nato e sono cresciuto nel settore giovanile, poi sono stato un po' in giro e infine sono tornato. Mi è stata affidata la fascia da capitano, quindi per me è un bel progetto che mi rende davvero soddisfatto. Ho avuto l'opportunità di andare in squadre magari più blasonate, che potevano lottare per posizioni più alte o per l'Europa, anche se nella vita non si può mai dire dove potremo arrivare: intanto ci godiamo questa vittoria. Sono molto contento del percorso che stiamo facendo; anche la società ha le idee chiare e sa cosa vuole fare, avendo scelto un allenatore coraggioso e una linea di mercato altrettanto coraggiosa. Siamo tanti giovani e tanti italiani, questo ci fa piacere: sono davvero orgoglioso di essere il capitano di questa squadra".

A proposito del tuo attaccamento al gruppo, abbiamo notato un bel gesto in occasione del calcio di rigore: hai preso tu il pallone in mano, ma poi lo hai ceduto a Varela. Non so quali fossero le gerarchie, ma cos'è successo esattamente? Te lo ha chiesto lui?

"È un'annotazione giusta. Diciamo che il battitore designato potevo essere io, però in questo caso mi ha fatto davvero piacere lasciare il pallone a un ragazzo che si sta giocando anche il titolo di capocannoniere del campionato. Non me lo ha chiesto lui: sono stato io a dirgli spontaneamente di calciarlo, e direi che le cose sono andate anche molto bene".

Colgate-Palmolive vs. McDonald's: Which Consumer Goods Stock Is a Better Buy for 2027?

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Two consumer goods company giants, one leaning on Walmart and the other on thousands of franchisees, offer very different routes to steady returns.

By Lawrence Rothman, CFA – Updated Nov 23, 2026 at 5:11PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Colgate-Palmolive ›

When market volatility rises, many investors turn toward large and established companies for stability. Choosing between Colgate-Palmolive (CL -0.79%) and McDonald's (MCD +1.15%) requires weighing a household goods leader against a global fast-food powerhouse.

Colgate-Palmolive focuses on essential household items like toothpaste and soap, offering stability through products people buy regardless of the economy. McDonald's dominates the quick-service restaurant industry by leveraging a massive franchise network that generates recurring rent and royalty income. While one provides daily necessities and the other offers affordable convenience, both represent mature businesses that investors often compare for long-term consistency.

Colgate-Palmolive operates as a global leader in segments like oral care, home care, and pet nutrition through brands such as Colgate and Hill's. These essential products are sold in over 200 countries, making the company a prominent name among consumer staples stocks. Investors should note that nearly 11% of its 2025 sales came from Walmart (WMT +1.27%), creating a customer concentration that gives the retailer significant bargaining power over trade discounts.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, revenue reached $21.3 billion, which represents a growth rate of approximately 5.9% compared to the prior 12-month period. The company reported net income of $2.2 billion during this same period as it navigated a complex global environment. This performance reflects top-line expansion across its various geographic regions even as it faces competition from smaller, digital-first brands.

McDonald's utilizes a franchise-heavy business model where roughly 95% of its 45,000 restaurants are managed by independent owners. These franchisees provide the company with steady streams of rent and royalties, which support a high net margin.

In the 12 months ended Sept. 30, revenue reached $27.8 billion, representing a growth rate of roughly 5.8% over the previous 12-month period. The company recorded net income of $9.0 billion. McDonald's reported a net margin of 32.4% during this time frame, illustrating the profitability inherent in its franchise-led structure.

Colgate-Palmolive faces significant risks from its global footprint, as geopolitical instability in regions like the Middle East can disrupt operations. The company also deals with intense competition from private labels. Additionally, evolving global regulations concerning environmental impact and product safety require ongoing investment to ensure compliance.

McDonald's depends on the financial health of its franchisees to execute its expansion plans, and any broad economic downturn could impact its royalty streams. The business is also susceptible to reputational damage from food safety incidents and recent class action lawsuits regarding product labeling and marketing claims.

McDonald's carries a lower Forward P/E, while Colgate-Palmolive appears more attractive based on its P/S ratio, which compares market value to sales over the past twelve months.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

While neither company is a fast grower, they have generated positive sales and earnings gains. That's positive for mature companies.

Additionally, both have made strong commitments to paying dividends to shareholders. That's an important component of their total returns. In fact, Colgate and McDonald's have increased payments annually for 63 and 50 straight years, respectively. That makes them Dividend Kings, or companies that have raised dividends for at least 50 consecutive years.

Both have sufficient earnings to support dividends, too. Colgate-Palmolive has a payout ratio, or dividends divided by earnings, of 82%. Meanwhile, McDonald's has a 59% payout ratio.

Turning to the dividend yields. McDonald's 2.8% yield surpasses Colgate's 2.2% yield.

The decision comes down to the underlying businesses and dividends. With McDonald's higher dividend yield and asset-light business conducive to free cash flow generation, that stock is my choice over Colgate-Palmolive.

Is Social Media Really the Great Equalizer? Here’s What Hulu’s ‘The Circle’ Host Tinx Has to Say (Exclusive)

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I sat down with Christina Najjar to talk alliances, authenticity, and the responsibility of being the “internet’s big sister.”

After a seven-season run on Netflix, The Circle has found a new home on Hulu, and things look very, very different. Rather than “normies,” the cast is now made up of familiar faces from the Walt Disney Company reality TV ecosystem and micro-influencers disguised as civilians. (Even the player with the most modest initial following, Cyrus Irani, had over 10,000 followers at the time his casting was announced. Is it a problem? Not necessarily. But it’s certainly a switch-up from season 1’s social media skeptic Shubham Goel.)

The revival series is also airing in near-real time, with new episodes dropping Tuesday through Thursday and audience voting being used to shape the process. And perhaps the biggest change: stepping into hosting duties in place of comedian and actor Michelle Buteau is SiriusXM’s Christina “Tinx” Najjar, who rose to fame on social media during the COVID-19 pandemic.

As a longtime Circle devotee, I was...curious about what these changes might mean for my favorite competition series. So when I got the chance to fly to Atlanta to visit the set (and meet Tinx) myself, I took it. There I chatted with her about the power of social media, the potential problem of having celebs in the Circle, and the player she thinks might win it all.

I truly couldn’t believe it. I know how many loyal fans The Circle has, so when I got the call that I got it—I mean, I’m still pinching myself every day. It’s a really big moment for me, and I know I have big shoes to fill. And I really just want to make everybody proud.

I remember watching the very first episode and thinking, “Wow, this is such a fresh concept, and it’s truly a social experiment for the modern age.” So the fact that I’m here all these years later is truly a pinch-me moment.

I relate to it so much, because in COVID, I was living in a studio apartment in L.A. I had just moved. I didn’t have a lot of friends, and all I had was my phone. That’s why I initially picked up TikTok and thought, “Who knows what’s going to happen in the world? I might as well make a few videos and try to make friends online.” And as soon as I started creating content, I just realized, “Wow, I love connecting with people. I love entertaining people.” It’s been a wild six years ever since.

I grew up doing theater in high school, but honestly, no. I truthfully had a lot of limiting beliefs about myself. I just thought there was no way I could ever be an influencer. I didn’t feel pretty enough. I didn’t think I was put together enough. And I think one thing COVID taught us is that anybody who has a dream can go for it. Social media is really the great equalizer in that sense, because if you are funny, if you have something to say, you can get online and you can connect with people.

It’s such an honor, and I love that people look up to me for advice. It’s the honor of my life that people trust me enough to call me on my radio show for advice or write to me, and I just try to be a voice of reason and remind women to know their worth. I feel like this is a journey I was meant to be on, and all of the opportunities that being online has afforded me, because it really has given me so much. The fact that I’m sitting here as the host of The Circle is—again, I can’t believe it.

It’s so different because I’m used to just holding up my phone, recording, cutting quickly, and pressing post. This is a totally different operation, but the crew is so cool, production is so cool, and the writers that I work with are just so smart and so funny. I was actually shocked when they explained to me the process of how close to the wire we are. It is as close to real time as you could ever think.

Because I have lived online for six years, I post in real time myself. I’m someone who wakes up and I’m like, “Hey guys, I’m going to the gym. Here’s what I’m eating for breakfast.” I don’t know if I would know how to do it any other way, because I’m just, like, an oversharer, so I think I would just be myself and see what happened. But I can be strategic. I feel like I would make a lot of alliances. I’m good at that.

I honestly think maybe the celebrities are at a slight disadvantage because, as you say, people have perceptions about them. I feel like they also have more of a target on their backs.

He was shocked. He had such a good strategy. I was honestly shocked he got out, too. He’s so likable, and everyone knew it was really him. He got got. He got got by Justin [Hinsley], if I’m being honest, who has very strong strategy. I think Justin is incredibly strategic and knows how to play the game.

I mean, Hannah [Brown], obviously. I don’t think she knows how to play any other way. She’s so genuine, and she’s definitely playing with her heart. So we’ll see how that fares and whether it’s strategy or heart that wins.

I think it’s totally fine, because there’s a lot on the line here. This is a popularity contest, but there’s a big prize at the end. So I think it’s fun when people are ruthless. Why not? It’s a game, and games are meant to be won.

Scan Walmart Clearance With the App – It Can Ring Up Below the Sticker

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

A Walmart clearance sticker keeps the price it was printed with, and the store may have marked the item down again since. Scan the item with the free Walmart app while you’re in the store. The app shows that store’s current price, and that’s what you’ll pay at the register.

Walmart says each store’s manager can cut prices to clear out extra stock, and that changing prices on paper tags by hand could take hours or even days. The register charges the new price in the meantime.

Walmart is replacing its paper shelf tags with digital labels that show the same price the register charges. It said in March 2026 that it expects every U.S. store to have them within a year. A clearance sticker on the item itself is still paper, though, so it’s the likeliest place to find an old price, along with the shelf tags in any store that hasn’t switched yet.

Say a pair of kids’ sneakers carries a $14.97 clearance sticker, and the store has since cut the price to $5. The sticker still says $14.97, but the app shows $5, and $5 is what you’ll pay.

Look for clearance stickers on the displays at the ends of aisles, along the back wall of each department, and in the seasonal aisle the week after a holiday. Open the app inside the store with location sharing on, since it shows in-store prices only once it knows you’re there. Then tap the scanner icon in the search bar and scan the product’s own barcode, not one printed for shipping or storage.

The price you see applies only at that store. Walmart says prices can vary from store to store and that it doesn’t price match its own stores, so the store won’t match a lower price you found at another Walmart. Clearance is also whatever the store has left, so an item you pass up today may be gone the next time you come in.

For a big purchase you can time, like a TV or a mattress, skip the clearance hunt and wait for its category’s yearly sale season.

Scan every clearance sticker before the item goes in your cart, and buy what scans at a price you’d pay.

Download the free Walmart app and sign in before your next trip.

Tuya Reports Third Quarter 2026 Unaudited Financial Results and Declaration of Cash Dividend

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SANTA CLARA, Calif., Nov. 23, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Tuya Inc. ("Tuya" or the "Company") (NYSE: TUYA; HKEX: 2391), a global leading AI cloud platform service provider, today announced its unaudited financial results for the third quarter ended September 30, 2026 and the declaration of a cash dividend.

For further information on the non-GAAP financial measures presented above, see the section headed "Use of Non-GAAP Financial Measures."

Mr. Xueji (Jerry) Wang, Founder and Chief Executive Officer of Tuya, commented, "In the third quarter, opportunities and challenges coexisted in the global consumer electronics industry. On the one hand, AI-native hardware continued to move from concept toward deployment at scale. New categories such as companion robots and AI toys rose rapidly, becoming a new engine for industry growth, which fully demonstrated that users' new demands for hardware in the AI era have begun to emerge. On the other hand, upstream component costs such as memory chips were still rising, and demand in traditional categories also faced pressure. Against this backdrop, we still maintained solid growth, with total revenue increasing by 19.5% year over year to US$98.6 million. The growth rate further accelerated compared to the second quarter, and we achieved year-over-year growth for the 13th consecutive quarter. In particular, PaaS revenue grew by 18.3% year over year, reflecting the continued increase in penetration of smart products across different regions and categories.

We continued to fully embrace AI and accelerated the promotion of ecosystem deployment. During the quarter, shipments of new AI-native categories grew by over 400% year over year. We debuted the AI home companion robot Doova at IFA in Berlin and the Global Digital Trade Expo. With the AI life assistant Hey Tuya as a core entry point, we integrated the three major application ecosystems of AI Home, AI Robot, and AI Energy, as well as the full-stack Matter combo solution. At the same time, subscription businesses such as door lock value-added services and pet cloud storage developed steadily, driving a continuous increase in the proportion of recurring revenue. Regarding developer tools, following Tuya Cobuilder, we launched the AI-native no-code application development platform Tuya AI Coding this quarter, extending developer tools from hardware development to application development. Looking ahead, we will focus on the three major scenarios mentioned above to promote large-scale shipments and the commercialization of AI-native hardware such as companion robots, pet care devices, and home energy management devices, and continue to improve the developer toolchain, Agent orchestration, and commercialization capabilities."

Mr. Yi (Alex) Yang, Director and Chief Financial Officer of Tuya, added, "In the third quarter, all three business segments delivered year-over-year revenue growth, with total revenue increasing by 19.5% year over year to US$98.6 million. As a foundation for our growth, the PaaS business saw its revenue increase by 18.3% year over year to US$70.1 million; Premium PaaS customers contributed approximately 89.6% of the PaaS revenue, and the core customer base remained stable. Smart home & robot product revenue grew by 26.7% year over year to US$15.0 million, primarily benefiting from growth in demand for categories such as AI audio and video, AI companion toys, and energy. AI application & others revenue increased by 18.6% year over year to US$13.6 million, primarily driven by the growth in cloud services, AI video cloud storage, and AI value-added services."

Third Quarter 2026 Unaudited Financial Results

Total revenue in the third quarter of 2026 increased by 19.5% to US$98.6 million from US$82.5 million in the same period of 2025.

Total gross profit in the third quarter of 2026 increased by 13.6% to US$45.2 million from US$39.8 million in the same period of 2025. The gross margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 45.8%, compared to 48.3% in the same period of 2025.

Gross margin fluctuated primarily due to changes in product and solution mix, as well as fluctuations in semiconductor supply-chain pricing. As an AI developer platform with a rich ecosystem of smart devices and applications, the Company remains focused on AI offering with compelling value propositions while maintaining economic efficiency.

Operating expenses increased by 0.8% to US$36.3 million in the third quarter of 2026 from US$36.0 million in the same period of 2025. Non-GAAP operating expenses increased by 15.9% to US$35.8 million in the third quarter of 2026 from US$30.9 million in the same period of 2025. For further information on the non-GAAP financial measures presented above, see the section headed "Use of Non-GAAP Financial Measures."

PROFIT FROM OPERATIONS AND OPERATING MARGIN

Profit from operations in the third quarter of 2026 was US$8.9 million, compared to US$3.8 million in the same period of 2025. The Company had a non-GAAP profit from operations of US$9.4 million in the third quarter of 2026, compared to US$8.9 million in the same period of 2025, demonstrating consistent operating profitability and leverage.

Operating margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 9.0%, up 4.4 percentage points from 4.6% in the same period of 2025. Non-GAAP operating margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 9.5%, down 1.3 percentage points from 10.8% in the same period of 2025.

Net profit in the third quarter of 2026 was US$18.5 million, an increase of 23.8% from US$15.0 million in the same period of 2025. Non-GAAP net profit in the third quarter of 2026 was US$19.1 million, compared to US$20.1 million in the same period of 2025.

Net margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 18.8%, an improvement of 0.6 percentage points from 18.2% in the same period of 2025. Non-GAAP net margin in the third quarter of 2026 was 19.3%, compared to 24.4% in the same period of 2025.

Basic and diluted net profit per ADS was US$0.03 in the third quarter of 2026, compared to US$0.02 in the same period of 2025. Each ADS represents one Class A ordinary share.

Non-GAAP basic and diluted net profit per ADS was US$0.03 in the third quarter of 2026, compared to US$0.03 in the same period of 2025.

CASH AND CASH EQUIVALENTS, TIME DEPOSITS AND TREASURY SECURITIES RECORDED AS SHORT-TERM AND LONG-TERM INVESTMENTS

Cash and cash equivalents, time deposits and treasury securities recorded as short-term and long-term investments were US$971.9 million as of September 30, 2026, compared to US$1,017.3 million as of December 31, 2025. The Company believes its current cash position is sufficient to meet its current liquidity and working capital needs.

NET CASH GENERATED FROM OPERATING ACTIVITIES

Net cash generated from operating activities in the third quarter of 2026 was US$12.7 million, compared to US$30.0 million in the same period of 2025. The net cash generated from operating activities for the third quarter of 2026 was mainly attributable to working capital changes in the ordinary course of business.

For further information on non-GAAP financial measures presented above, see the section headed "Use of Non-GAAP Financial Measures."

Currently, innovative AI hardware is in a rapid ramp-up phase, but the overall macroeconomic environment remains complex. On the one hand, AI-native hardware is moving from concept toward deployment at scale, with new categories such as companion robots and AI toys rising rapidly, becoming a new engine for industry growth. On the other hand, upstream component costs such as memory chips continue to rise, demand recovery in certain traditional categories is relatively slow, and uncertainties relating to trade policies and supply chains remain. Performance varies across regions, categories and markets. With the accelerated integration of AI and consumer hardware, Physical AI scenarios are ushering in a broader space for development.

Against this backdrop, we will continue to advance our AI-driven strategy. At the product level, we will focus on three high-potential scenarios, namely AI Home, AI Energy and AI Robot, to promote large-scale shipments and the commercialization of AI-native hardware such as companion robots, home energy management devices, and pet care devices. At the developer platform level, we will continue to improve Tuya Cobuilder, Tuya AI Coding and Agent orchestration capabilities, further lowering the development threshold, and enabling more customers and brands to quickly launch their own AI applications based on the Tuya platform. At the user application level, we will continue to enhance the response efficiency and reliability of Hey Tuya, and advance subscription and value-added services such as AI-powered protection, AI energy saving, pet care, and video understanding, to enhance our renewal and ongoing service capabilities.

Looking ahead, we will maintain disciplined execution, and continuously invest in AI R&D and platform capabilities. We will focus on AI-native applications, Physical AI scenarios, and developer platform construction, leveraging the advantages of our platform, device ecosystem and global developer base to transform AI technology from a tool capability into real and scalable commercial value. At the same time, we also recognize that our future performance may be affected by changes in consumer demand, regional economic conditions, inventory cycles, component and logistics costs, foreign exchange and interest-rate volatility, tariffs and trade-policy adjustments, and broader geopolitical uncertainties.

The aggregate amount of the Cash Dividend will be approximately US$35 million, which is payable in U.S. dollars and in cash, and will be funded by surplus cash and to be paid out from the share premium account of the Company. The determination to make distributions and the amount of such distributions will be made at the discretion of its Board and will be based upon the Company's operations and earnings, including, but not limited to, considerations of the Company's GAAP and Non-GAAP net profits, cash flows, financial conditions and other relevant factors.
